### Stage C4a small gaps before arithmetic (branch p39-c4a-small-gaps, 2026-11-27, not committed by the coder)
Item 0 (stable gate): `tests/test_controller_store_import_large.py::test_shutdown_store_while_job_running` no longer races two 5 s clocks: `ProjectStore.request_cancel` is wrapped so the job continues when it is told to cancel (the 10 s wait in the job is only a hang guard), `shutdown_store(timeout=10.0)`, the start loops are bounded turn counts plus `started_event.wait(10.0)`, and, after the Team-Thinker review (F1), the test now holds the job on a `release_event` after it saw the cancel, runs `shutdown_store` in a thread, asserts the thread is STILL ALIVE after a bounded `join(0.5)` (a negative check that cannot flake under load), then releases the job and asserts the result is `(True, job already ended)` and that `request_cancel` woke the job. The first version of this test (the one that only asserted `job_ended` after `shutdown_store` returned) did NOT kill the mutants "wait loop breaks unconditionally" and "never calls request_cancel", because the import ends within milliseconds after the cancel; the rewrite kills "wait loop breaks", "if True: break", "returns True without waiting" and "request_cancel then return True" by the assertion "shutdown_store returned while the import job was still held (it did not wait)". The mutant "never calls request_cancel" is equivalent at controller level (the controller cancels the job first and `StoreJob.cancel` calls `ProjectStore.request_cancel`, jobs.py:104), so it is killed one level down by the new `tests/test_store_session.py::test_shutdown_requests_the_cancel_and_waits_for_a_held_job` (assertion "shutdown() did not request the cancel"); that test kills all five shutdown mutants by assertion. 30 of 30 passes of both tests together under 10 CPU burners. C3b did NOT cause this flake: it fails on main 13120cb (before C3b) as well as on c6f9c86, measured by the Planner over 30 runs each with 10 busy-loop processes (the fail counts were not in the message the coder received). Wall-clock assertions removed from `test_store_duckdb.py::test_speed_300k_rows`, `test_store_session.py` (3 tests), `test_store_filter_parity.py::test_filter_timing_on_the_small_file`, `test_store_stats.py::test_half_million_unique_rows_fast`, and `test_controller_store_real_app_300k.py` (the page and operation time limits; the times are printed).
Item 1 (own CALCULATE filter on ANOTHER column, grouped): `plan_group_by` refuses with `REASON_GROUP_OWN_FILTER` only when an own predicate touches the group column (new `own_predicate_columns`, a walk over NOT, || and IN, names case-folded); otherwise the own predicates go to the WHERE like a slicer. A group with no row under the own filter is dropped (the engine shows blank, the app drops blanks). Sweep family `group_own` (632 rows: 6 group columns x 8 aggregates x own filters on other columns; 504 same, 128 refused by the existing text-group and text-IN guards, 0 differs), `group_own_column` (10 rows, refused by name or by the whitelist), `group_own_context` (4 rows, own filter plus slicer). Measured: no empty-group 0-versus-blank difference, so no COALESCE or NULLIF was needed. Evidence (reviewer probe, not circular): the engine returns BLANK for COUNTROWS, SUM, COUNT, COUNTA, DISTINCTCOUNT, COUNTBLANK, AVERAGE, MIN and MAX on a group with no row under the own filter, and the plan drops such a group. The own-filter-on-group-column check inside NOT or || cannot be reached end to end (the whitelist allows NOT and || only over whole-number columns, which cannot be group columns): it is proved on parsed expressions. Plain sentence of `REASON_GROUP_OWN_FILTER` now says "on the column the results are grouped by". Three existing tests changed because the former refusal is now an answer: `tests/test_dax_sql.py::test_group_by_refusals_have_stable_reasons`, `tests/test_dax_route.py::test_route_grouped_own_sentences_and_no_type_names` and `test_filter_context_with_an_alias_chain_equals_the_engine_or_is_refused`, and the app test of V7 became V12 (refused) plus V11 (identical to the in-memory twin); V11 is also in `test_async_results_equal_the_in_memory_engine`.
Item 2: F1 `REASON_DATE_BEFORE_1900` in the date guard, same single scan as the unreadable-date check (`BOOL_OR` plus `MIN(TRY_CAST(...)) < DATE '1900-01-01'`), sweep column `sdt_old` with the cells 1899-12-30, 1900-01-01, 0001-01-01, 0100-05-05 (family `date_old`, 9 rows, 8 refused-guard, the plain group-by on another column stays same). F2 tests: scale-only change at equal digits (5,8 to 6,8) and a decimal column dropped from the table both give `REASON_PLAN_STALE`. F3: a dropped table gives `REASON_PLAN_STALE` from every guard query (IN guard, date guard, text-group space guard, decimal stamp); an interrupt, a closed store or any other store error still propagates (`is_missing_table_error` looks for "does not exist" or "Catalog Error" only). A plan with NO guard still raises StoreError from its main query on a dropped table (the route turns that into "the large table could not be read"; `tests/test_dax_route.py::test_dropped_table_is_a_refusal_not_an_exception` pins it). The decimal-stamp read (`column_decimal_meta`) has always swallowed every error, so an interrupt there is reported as stale and the route maps it to the timeout when its budget fired (unchanged). F4 not fixed, documented in the module docstring and in `test_contract_a_column_that_gains_decimals_needs_a_rebuilt_plan`.
Item 3 (decisions, not coded): (a) IN and `&&` on numeric and date columns with blank cells: the engine raises "Cannot convert value '' of type Text to type Number." and the store refuses the shapes by name (`REASON_IN_NUMERIC_MIX`, `REASON_LOGICAL_AND`, route patterns "Engine bug: numeric IN", "Engine bug: date IN filter"). Unchecked: the engine raises the message above for a blank cell; whether Power BI raises is not known (the Power BI answer check was not run), so whether this is an engine bug in `measures.py` or the correct answer is open. Order: first the Power BI answer check, then an engine task that fixes `measures.py` and locks the answers, then a store task that lifts the refusals with the sweep. Power BI answer check to run (not run here): `CALCULATE(COUNTROWS(t), t[num] IN {1, 2})`, `CALCULATE(COUNTROWS(t), t[date] IN {DATE(2024,1,1)})` and `CALCULATE(COUNTROWS(t), t[num] = 1 && t[num2] > 0)` on a table where `num`, `num2` and `date` have blank cells; expected from Power BI: no error, blanks never match. (b) Parquet plan: not in C4 (sizing in C4b.md, section "After C4").
New tests: `tests/test_dax_sql_c4a.py` (46 tests). New refusal reason: `REASON_DATE_BEFORE_1900`, in `_PLAN_CODE_REASONS` and `FRAGMENTS` of `dax_route`. UNCHECKED against Power BI: the sentence "large tables do not show the way Power BI does yet" and the rule itself come from the C1 finding, which compared the engine and the store, not Power BI. Consequence to know: a legitimate pre-1900 date column (for example a birth date) now refuses EVERY large-table measure that reads it (aggregate, predicate or group-by) until the rule is lifted.
Review fixes (Team-Thinker FAIL, narrow, 2026-11-27): F1 shutdown test rewritten (above). F2 `tests/test_controller_store_real_app_300k.py` has coarse hang guards again (50 s per operation, 10 s per page; about 10 times the last measured values, which are not speed claims): measured on this Mac: import 3.52 s, page reads 0.0 / 5.1 / 4.7 ms, export 2.21 s, sort amount asc 0.202 s, desc 0.188 s, sort code 0.056 s, filter category 0.029 s, filter id range 0.043 s, save-as 0.04 s. F3 `tests/test_csv_store_import.py` cancel latency: printed (0.064 s measured) plus a 10 s hang guard (the other `time.time()` asserts at about lines 818 and 1240 of that file were not named by the review and are unchanged). F4 new tests kill four more mutants: table-name case in `own_predicate_columns`, `own_columns` cut to the first column, a stamp compare that ignores digits, and the two date reasons sharing one text.

### Stage C4b arithmetic over totals on a large table (branch p40-c4b-arithmetic, 2026-11-27, uncommitted, for review)
Brief C4b steps 1 to 3 and the route are done; step 4 is done except the Power BI answer checks (not run). `+ - * /`, unary minus and `DIVIDE(a, b[, alt])` over the whitelisted totals now run on a large table, for cards, visuals (grouped), previews and validation; the controller needed NO change (`git diff` of `controller.py` is empty).
Design: nothing is translated to SQL arithmetic. `analytics_studio/store/dax_arith.py` (new) holds the pure combiner (`Num(kind, value)`, `combine`, `negate`, `divide`, `evaluate`, `bare_result`) and `parse_arith`, which turns the AST into a tree plus up to 8 distinct totals (leaves, each the DAX text of one total, written back and checked to parse to the same node). `dax_whitelist._classify_arith` classifies every leaf with the existing classifier (shape "ARITH"); `dax_sql.ArithPlan` runs one `MeasurePlan` per leaf (own guards and refusals, first Refusal wins) and combines; `dax_sql.GroupedArithPlan` runs one `GroupByPlan` per leaf plus a COUNTROWS plan for the keys. `dax_route._run_on_reader` uses a `grouped` attribute on the plan classes instead of `isinstance`.
Where the engine differed from the brief (the engine wins): (1) a measure REFERENCE has no static type in the engine (`_static_numeric_type` looks a name up as a column), so `[A] / [B]`, `[A] * 2` and `CALCULATE([A], p)` are computed in exact 28-digit Decimals: no double conversion, no Int64 wrap, a zero loses its sign; the combiner has a third kind "unknown" and a `("ref", ...)` tree node for this (the brief's `Qty Share = [Total Qty] / [Row Count]` is an exact quotient, `COUNTROWS(t) / 7` is the double). (2) The value a measure shows goes through `_bare_result` (zero of either sign is 0, NaN of either sign is NaN) while signs are kept inside an expression (`1 / (SUM(x) * 0 * -1)` is -Infinity): `bare_result` is applied at the end of both plans. (3) The static type of DIVIDE is that of the quotient (double, or unknown with an untyped operand). (4) A column of blank cells only is typed text by the import, so the brief's `SUM(t[sbl])` leaf does not exist (the blank leaf is `CALCULATE(SUM(t[sd]), t[scat] = "ZZZ")`, plus a blank Int64 leaf in the family `arith_blank_int`). (5) Sweep columns hold at most 15-digit whole numbers (16 digits or more are typed text), so family H squares and multiplies instead of summing values near 2**62. (6) `tools/store_engine_diff.py` `with_filter_context` wrapped `CALCULATE(a) / CALCULATE(b)` as ONE call and put the slicer on the last total only (a silent wrong engine reference for any quotient of two CALCULATEs); it now checks that the whole text is one call.
Decisions to know: (a) grouped keys: the brief asked for identical key lists, which would refuse a share by product (own filter) as "stale" because the C4a join drops groups with no row for the own filter; the plan takes the keys and order from a COUNTROWS plan (the engine's VALUES) and reads a key a total does not return as blank; a key a total returns that the COUNTROWS plan lacks, or a repeated key, is `REASON_PLAN_STALE`. (b) Non-finite group values: the in-memory twin draws Infinity and NaN groups, so the store returns the same floats (`V15 zero div`, `V16 nan by region` identical to the twin); no refusal was needed and `REASON_ARITH_NON_FINITE_GROUP` was removed. (c) `CALCULATE([arithmetic measure], filters)` is refused by name (`REASON_ARITH_IN_CALCULATE`); `CALCULATE([M])` with no filter is `[M]`. (d) Depth cap 8 counts a left chain `a+b+c+...` level by level: `(a1+...+a8)/b` is refused as "nested more than 8 levels deep" and a balanced sum of 8 totals plus a divisor passes (guess: a reviewer may want the cap to count only different operators; the cap exists for the recursive walk). (e) A whole-number total outside the signed 64 bit range gives `REASON_ARITH_WHOLE_BEYOND_64` at execute (stub-tested: a small table cannot reach it); DIVIDE of an Infinity or NaN gives `REASON_ARITH_DIVIDE_NON_FINITE`. (f) An unguarded leaf (a plain whole-number SUM) still raises StoreError from its own query on a dropped table, as a single total does today; a guarded leaf gives `REASON_PLAN_STALE`.
New reasons (all with a plain sentence in `dax_route.FRAGMENTS`; the two execute-time ones are plan-coded): `REASON_ARITH_LEAF`, `_DATE`, `_LITERAL`, `_DIVIDE_ALT`, `_CAP_LEAVES`, `_CAP_NODES`, `_CAP_DEPTH`, `_IN_CALCULATE`, `_WHOLE_BEYOND_64`, `_DIVIDE_NON_FINITE`; two parser texts mapped in `_PROBLEMS` ("DIVIDE needs a numerator ..." and "DIVIDE alternate result must be a constant ...").
Rules tool: `build_sweep_catalog(arithmetic=True)` and `run_sweep(arithmetic=True)` (the command line `--sweep` always does) add 3,201 rows in 14 families (`arith_*`, counts in the baseline key `arith_family_counts`, checked by `evaluate_sweep_policy`), compared with `values_identical` (class, value at tolerance 0, sign of zero, sign of NaN, report text); the default catalog and the old tests are unchanged. Result before fix 1 (superseded: see "C4b fix 1" below): `--sweep` exit 0 at 300 rows seeds 1 to 20 and 1000 rows seed 42: same=4430, differs=0, refused 722. `--plan --rows 2000 --seed 42`: allowed 603, plan-same 603, differs 0, refused-allowed 0; the five old arithmetic catalog formulas are now allowed and exact (four catalog shapes renamed to "ARITH").
Existing tests changed (necessarily): `tests/test_dax_whitelist.py` (the four named rows and `DIVIDE(SUM(t[w]), 2)` moved to the allowed tables, new refused rows, catalog counts 570/244 to 575/239, Rule B gaps, `gaps["arithmetic"]` 7 to 2, three `SWEEP_REFUSED` rows `+ 1`, `1 +`, unary minus moved), `tests/test_dax_sql.py` (the refusal entry now `^`; Rule A+ counts 570/244 to 575/239), `tests/test_dax_route.py` (the refusal entry now `^`; the runtime battery gained 27 arithmetic expressions and 5 chains), `tests/test_controller_dax_route_app.py` (CATALOG 34 to 48, REFUSED, `RATIO` allowed and `REFUSED_MEASURE` = ROUND, the 8-visual test is the 14-visual test, two hard-coded `== 5` counts now `len(VISUAL_MEASURES)`), `tests/test_controller_dax_async.py` (V5, V13, V14 in the identity test, one arithmetic card in the key test). New: `tests/test_dax_arith.py` (69), `test_dax_arith_plan.py` (125), `test_dax_arith_route.py` (77), `test_dax_arith_sweep.py` (42), one real-app test (create, edit, preview, 8-total cap, query count).
Measured: 48 of 48 card texts and 14 of 14 visuals identical to the twin at 300,000 rows; 49 cards refresh with 178 reader queries (3.6 per card) and 0 on the main connection; an 8-total measure is validated and created in 3.07 s at 300,000 rows. 55 source mutants applied by hand (36 combiner and parser, 16 planner and whitelist, 3 route): all killed by named tests (two survived at first, a typed zero of an untyped operator and CALCULATE of a measure, and got tests). NOT done: the three Power BI answer checks of the brief (a 20,000-row total of doubles, an Infinity or NaN group in a bar chart, a blank numerator with an alternate in a visual); the engine rules are the in-memory engine's, which the saved Power BI answers cover on small tables, and measure references in arithmetic (exact Decimals, no double) are the engine's behaviour, unchecked against Power BI (guess: Power BI types a measure by its expression, so `[Total Qty] / [Row Count]` may be a double there).
C4b fix 1 (Team-Thinker TASK 16 review: verdict FAIL, narrow; uncommitted, not gated). (1) Silent wrong value: a `/` or DIVIDE whose divisor contains MIN or MAX of a column is refused by name (`REASON_ARITH_SIGNED_ZERO_DIVISOR`). The store returns an unsigned 0 where the engine keeps a minus zero: `COUNTROWS(t) / MIN(t[sw])` on "-0" cells gave -Infinity in the engine and Infinity in the plan. A literal `/ 0` stays allowed. (2) Torn read: an arithmetic plan reads `store.table_version` of each physical table it reads (`read_tables`) before and after its statements and returns `REASON_PLAN_STALE` if one changed. `StoreReader.table_version` delegates to the owner store. Test seam `dax_sql.ARITH_BETWEEN_LEAF_QUERIES` is None by default. (3) DIVIDE by a literal with 0 < |x| < 2.2250738585072014E-308 is refused by name (`REASON_ARITH_DIVIDE_SUBNORMAL`). (4) Tests for the five survivors (underflow, overflow, DIVIDE overflow, grouped values through `bare_result`, plan code of `REASON_ARITH_WHOLE_BEYOND_64`). The refusal test of the route runs no value query (spy reader). (5) Depth-cap sentence now says "more than 8 terms in a row or nested more than 8 levels deep" (the constant keeps its name). (6) Sweep: the dataset holds "-0" and "-0.00" decimal cells. The refused set depends on the row count (guard refusals come from the generated rows), so `tools/store_engine_diff_baseline.json` has `sweep_refused_by_rows` and the policy checks the set of the requested row count. Measured, differs 0 everywhere: `--sweep` exit 0 at 5 rows (seeds 2 and 5; refused 484), 12 rows (seed 42; 558), 40 rows (seed 42; 836), 300 rows (seeds 1 to 5; 836 each), 1000 rows (seed 42; 836). The mutant scripts used in the review are not in the repo; they are in the scratch folder outside it. Power BI answer checks NOT run yet: (a) totals of doubles over 20,000 rows printed with 15 or more digits; (b) an Infinity or NaN group in a bar chart; (c) `DIVIDE(blank numerator, 0, 7)` in a visual; (d) whether a measure reference is typed (double) or exact in Power BI, e.g. `[Total Qty] / [Row Count]`; (e) the sign of zero in `1 / MIN(col)` with a "-0.00" cell; (f) DIVIDE with a denominator below 2.2E-308.

C4b fix 2 (Team-Thinker TASK 17 review of fix 1: verdict FAIL, narrow; fixed by the Sonnet fixer; uncommitted, not gated). (F1) The text rule for a MIN or MAX divisor is gone (it refused every `/ MIN(col)`, which over-blocked 114 sweep rows, and it missed `/ CALCULATE([MinM], ...)`). Now an EXECUTE-TIME check: the parser marks each total that is a MIN or MAX of a column (`ArithLeaf.min_max`, read from the tree: MIN or MAX call, CALCULATE of one, a measure reference followed through measures; the mark travels on the leaf `Num.min_max`, not compared); `dax_arith.evaluate` raises `ArithUnsupported(REASON_ARITH_SIGNED_ZERO_DIVISOR)` for a `/` whose divisor subtree reads such a total (through ref, neg, bin and DIVIDE nodes) and whose value is zero of either sign. The sentence is now "dividing by a minimum or maximum that is zero: the sign of that zero may differ from Power BI"; the route maps it as a plan reason (code "plan"). A non-zero divisor is computed as before. DIVIDE is NOT refused on a zero divisor (deviation from the brief, measured): DIVIDE answers a zero of either sign with the alternate, so the sign never reaches the value (`DIVIDE(COUNTROWS(t), MIN(t[sw]), 7)` on "-0" cells: engine 7, plan 7). Grouped: one zero total in any group refuses the whole measure. (F3) A number with 0 < |x| < 2.2250738585072014E-308 is refused by name ANYWHERE in the arithmetic (in the literal handler, including DIVIDE alternates and measure bodies), so `x / 1E-310` is refused too (coverage cost, simpler than agreeing case by case); 2.2250738585072014E-308 and 1E-324 (zero as a double) stay allowed. The reason sentence is reworded ("a number smaller than the smallest normal double in the arithmetic is not on the store whitelist"; constant name unchanged). (F2) `StoreReader.table_version` is tested through the real reader (a stub-free test; mutant "returns 0" is killed). The append path bumps the token before the COMMIT as well as after; that alone does NOT close the window (a reader whose first token read falls between the first bump and the COMMIT sees the same token after a lagging second bump: reproduced deterministically in `test_rows_that_land_while_the_second_token_bump_waits_are_stale_not_a_torn_value`), so `DuckStore.table_writing(name)` (also on `StoreReader`) is True from just before the COMMIT until both bumps are done, and a plan refuses as stale if the flag was set at its first read or a token moved. `append_rows` now moves the token by two (the contract text says so; existing tests only assert "greater"). Lag probe (append with a 0.3 s lagging bump, 20 s, scalar and grouped): before, 1 torn value (0.9756...) in one run; after, 0 torn values in 4 runs (stale refusals only). Note: an append that passed its "refuse while a reader is open" check just before a reader opened is the only real way to reach this window (a reader opened first makes the append raise StoreBusyError); that check-then-open race itself is not changed. (4) Sweep: new families `arith_min_max` (101 rows: zero and non-zero MIN and MAX divisors in `/` and DIVIDE, through measures, CALCULATE of a measure, filtered to a zero MIN, grouped by `scat` and `sb`, with a slicer) and `arith_subnormal` (15 rows); counts are in `arith_family_counts`. Pins (SUPERSEDED by fix 3 below: the 40 pin and the zero-divisor rows are no longer pinned): `sweep_refused_by_rows` has exact sets for 40 (seed 42, 749 rows), 300 (seeds 1 to 5, the same 747 rows each) and 1000 (seed 42, the same 747 rows); the 5 and 12 entries are gone. Below 40 rows the policy is a subset rule for any seed: a refused row is in the 300-row set OR is a guard refusal for a zero MIN or MAX divisor (a small table reaches a zero MIN far more often, so strict "subset of the 300 set" from the brief could not hold; it is the same rule with the named zero-divisor refusals added), and every other row must be "same". The 300-row refused count is back to 722 for the rows that existed before fix 1 (the 114 over-blocked rows are "same" again) plus 25 refusals in the new families. `tests/test_dax_arith_sweep.py` line 156 is no longer relaxed: at seed 1 and 300 rows each family's refused set must equal its pinned rows and every other row must be "same". Measured, differs 0: `--sweep` exit 0 at 5 rows seeds 1 to 6, 12 rows seeds 1, 2 and 7, 20 rows seed 3, 40 rows seed 42, 300 rows seeds 1 to 5, 1000 rows seed 42; `--plan --rows 2000 --seed 42` exit 0 (allowed 603, plan-same 603, differs 0). Power BI answer check (NOT run; to be run by the Planner): `1 / CALCULATE(MIN(col), ...)` with a "-0.00" cell, for (a) a measure divisor `COUNTROWS(t) / [MinM]` and (b) a filtered measure divisor `COUNTROWS(t) / CALCULATE([MinM], t[scat] = "A")`: does Power BI show -Infinity (the engine does) or Infinity? Until then every zero MIN or MAX divisor is refused, so the sign question never reaches a value. Also open: a zero MIN or MAX that is not a divisor shows as 0 in both (same). Mutant scripts are in the scratch folder, not in the repo.

C4b fix 3 (Team-Thinker TASK 18 finding on fix 2: the exact per-size sweep pin was not seed independent; uncommitted, not gated). Cause: a zero MIN or MAX divisor refusal is found at execute time from the values, so which rows hit it depends on the generated rows (rows 40 seed 3: 1 row; found by the review), and the data guards (non-ASCII space, accented letters, date before 1900) fire only when the generated cells hold such a value (rows 41 seed 5: 37 pinned static refusals did not fire, the rows were "same" and equal to the engine). Fix, in `evaluate_sweep_policy`: (1) a refusal with `REASON_ARITH_SIGNED_ZERO_DIVISOR` (status refused-guard) is allowed at every size and seed and is not pinned; (2) the pinned sets in `tools/store_engine_diff_baseline.json` now hold only the static refusals (736 rows, equal at 300 rows seed 1 and 1000 rows seed 42); (3) below `SWEEP_EXACT_MIN_ROWS` = 150 rows a refused row must be in the 300-row set (a pinned refusal that did not fire leaves a row "same"; this is the seed independent form); from 150 rows the static set is pinned exactly per row count (300 and 1000 entries; any other count against the 300-row set). The "40" entry of fix 2 is removed: an exact pin at 40 rows cannot be seed independent (measured at 41 rows seed 5); this is the one deviation from "keep the exact pin at 40". Every non-refused row must be "same" and differs 0 still exits 1; a refusal with another reason outside the pinned set exits 1 at every size. Item 2: `create_table`, `drop_table` and `create_table_from_arrow` now flag the table as being written (`DuckStore._flagged`, cleared on any exit) from just before the statement or COMMIT until the bump is done, like `append_rows`. The seed-independence claim means exactly the sizes and seeds listed in the sweep table of this fix (all exit 0); other sizes and seeds are not claimed.
